- language / task / timestamp / other control tokens are excluded from the default CTC label set
- CTC `blank` is added by this project on top of the Whisper text-token vocabulary
- this gives:
  - no `<unk>` / OOV behavior for normal UTF-8 text
  - a tokenizer that is not tightly coupled to current training-set growth
  - a much smaller CTC head than Qwen / Gemma class LLM tokenizers
- `Qwen3 tokenizer.json` support is allowed as an ablation / comparison path, but it is not the default:
  - it uses a much larger CTC head than Whisper multilingual
  - it tends to merge multiple Chinese characters into one token, which can hurt CTC timestamp granularity
  - 4 x 4090 baseline configs must therefore start from a smaller token/frame budget than Whisper runs

YAML config policy:
- training must emit at least:
  - `model_config.yaml`
  - `train_config.yaml`
- public repository packaging must:
  - keep `README.md` aligned with the current implemented slices and open gaps
  - keep `ROADMAP.md` aligned with the public GitHub task breakdown for remaining milestones
  - document latest public training summary without committing checkpoints or run artifacts
  - exclude `runs/`, DeepSpeed states, exported weights, and local datasets from version control
- training CLI should also accept a YAML config file as the primary launch interface
- when both YAML and CLI args are present, only explicitly provided CLI args may override YAML fields
- DeepSpeed multi-GPU training should use the same YAML-first interface
- training YAML may embed a `deepspeed` mapping directly instead of forcing a separate JSON file
- prediction / evaluation should prefer reconstructing model architecture from YAML rather than repeated CLI shape arguments
- standalone prediction should live under a separate `predict` module / CLI and must not depend on training-loop entrypoints
- repository should also provide a labeled prediction CLI for dev/eval inspection:
  - consume manifest or WebDataset eval samples with references
  - run configurable CTC prefix beam search
  - support decode-time insertion / length bonus ablations
  - save `pred_text`, `ref_text`, token ids, and token-level alignments to JSONL
  - optionally save per-utterance decode diagnostics so short-hypothesis failures can be distinguished from frontend truncation bugs
  - support a small-sample preview workflow before running full WER/CER
- Rust deployment tooling should live under `tools/`; the first `Candle` predictor may target exported `CTC logits + lengths` rather than full RWKV encoder parity
- exported inference checkpoints should support:
  - `.pt` for training resume
  - `.safetensors` for inference loading in Python and future Rust model loading
- decode-export artifacts should support:
  - batched `part-xxxxx.safetensors` files with `logits` and `lengths`
  - sidecar `part-xxxxx.utt_ids.txt`
  - an index JSON summarizing exported parts, mode, blank id, frontend type, and timestamp projection config
- manual CLI shape arguments remain only as a fallback path
- repository should ship minimal example YAML files for training and model architecture so configs can be exchanged directly

Training stages:
1. short/mid-form bidirectional base training
2. fixed-rate Direction Dropout training for dual-mode robustness
3. long-form fine-tuning with concatenated or naturally long utterances
4. streaming/chunk robustness validation in `L2R` mode
5. offline evaluation in `Bi` and `Alt` modes

### 6.2 Optimization

Baseline optimizer rules derived from upstream:
- AdamW or DeepSpeed fused Adam
- `att.w0` uses `2x` LR scale
- weight decay only on large matrix weights
- bf16 training
- gradient checkpointing optional

DeepSpeed mainline policy for target 4090 training:
- optimizer should use `DeepSpeedCPUAdam` in AdamW mode
- default ZeRO mode should be `ZeRO-2`
- optimizer state should use `cpu offload`
- encoder activations should enable gradient checkpointing by default to maximize usable context / batch
- rank0 training output should include live progress, ETA, and per-step timing because audio decode / fbank extraction is online and the first batch can be much slower than steady-state
- variable-length training should use a `batch token budget` policy rather than blindly fixing `batch_size`
- the practical budget metric should be `padded_audio_tokens = B * max_audio_frames`, plus `text_tokens = sum(target_lengths)`
- training should log the observed batch token count, peak memory, and a `22 GiB` target-budget estimate from real runs
- when a candidate loader batch exceeds the configured token budget, the safe default is to shrink the effective local batch from `B` to the largest budget-compliant prefix `B'` for that step
- under `DDP` / `DeepSpeed`, token-budget enforcement must happen before the model step at batch-construction time; per-rank variable counts of forward/backward calls inside one logical step are not safe
- do not execute the dropped tail samples from an oversized candidate batch inside the same logical step
- we should not truncate audio time `T` against a full transcript by default, because that breaks CTC alignment semantics unless transcript segmentation is also available
- pathological single samples larger than the token budget should be isolated or skipped with an explicit warning rather than causing an OOM crash

Initial defaults:
- `lr = 4e-4` to `6e-4` range for base runs
- warmup enabled
- ZeRO-2 first, ZeRO-3 only if memory forces it
- DeepSpeed training YAML should normalize to:
  - `zero_optimization.stage = 2`
  - `zero_optimization.offload_optimizer.device = cpu`
  - `zero_optimization.offload_optimizer.pin_memory = true`

Direction Dropout for paper reproduction:
- The paper describes DirDrop as dropping one recurrent direction with a set probability.
- The experiment section states `For DirDrop, we use a dropout rate of 20%.`
- Historical strict reproduction configs therefore used:
  - `direction_variant = drop_both`
  - `p_start = 0.2`
  - `p_max = 0.2`
  - `warmup_steps = 0`
  - `ramp_steps = 0`
- This is no longer an active default for the current stabilization and Stage11+ ASR training line. Current training must keep full bidirectional encoder masks with `direction_variant = none`, `p_start = 0.0`, `p_max = 0.0`, `warmup_steps = 0`, and `ramp_steps = 0`; old reproduction configs are historical only unless a future ablation explicitly re-enables DirDrop in this spec and uses a separate run name.
- Scheduler-based ramping can remain in code only as a non-default ablation path.

Why not reuse upstream Lightning trainer:
- `train_temp` is tailored to LM training and old Lightning.
- This project needs ASR losses, variable-length audio batching, chunked validation, and simpler maintainability.
- We should reuse the optimization policy, not the full framework.

### 6.3 Hardware Plan

Target environment:
- `4 x RTX 4090`
- `torch 2.10`
- `CUDA 13.0`
- `DeepSpeed`
- managed by `uv`

Execution policy:
- keep all project dependencies in `pyproject.toml`
- use `uv` virtualenv and lockfile when environment sync becomes possible
- compile the fused CUDA extension against the active torch / CUDA toolchain
- default distributed launcher path is `deepspeed --num_gpus ... rwkvasr-train-ctc-deepspeed --config-yaml ...`
- when using `DeepSpeedCPUAdam` under a `torch cu128 + system CUDA 13.0` environment, allow `DS_SKIP_CUDA_CHECK=1` for the CPU optimizer build path
- training artifacts should include:
  - `model_config.yaml`
  - `train_config.yaml`
  - `deepspeed_config.yaml`
  - DeepSpeed checkpoint directories by step tag
  - a plain inference-friendly `step-<N>.pt` checkpoint when the active ZeRO stage allows direct export

## 7. Evaluation Plan

Required validation axes:
- CTC loss decreases on 1-batch overfit
- offline greedy decode sanity in `Bi` mode
- offline prefix beam search sanity with configurable `beam_size`
- Rust/Candle prefix beam search sanity from exported `safetensors logits`
- token-level CTC time alignment sanity in both Python and Rust predictors
- streaming chunked decode sanity in `L2R` mode
- same-checkpoint `Bi / L2R / Alt` decode sanity
- WER/CER on dev set
- latency and throughput under:
  - batch size 1
  - streaming chunk sizes
  - offline long-form chunks

Minimum benchmark outputs:
- tokens or frames per second during training
- audio seconds per second during inference
- peak GPU memory
- WER by chunk size
- WER delta between `Bi` and `L2R` from the same checkpoint
- WER delta between `Bi` and `Alt` from the same checkpoint

- add frontend, tokenizer integration, CTC projection, loss

  - Qwen3-ASR audio self-attention is not used; every attention slot is replaced with bidirectional RWKV-7 TimeMixer
  - Qwen3-ASR non-attention module names should be kept close enough to support partial warm-start from the official audio encoder for Conv2D, LayerNorm, FFN, and output projection weights
  - encoder hidden dimension and encoder output dimension may differ, so CTC and AR audio-conditioning heads must consume the encoder output dimension rather than assuming `n_embd`

### Phase 6: Training System
- manifest dataset
- collator
- DeepSpeed training entrypoint
- checkpoint save/load

Status:
- in progress
- upstream-style optimizer parameter grouping implemented
- Direction Dropout integrated into a minimal CTC training wrapper
- synthetic one-batch overfit runner implemented and validated
- JSONL manifest dataset, collator, checkpoint save/load, and a minimal train CLI implemented
- DeepSpeed entrypoint not implemented yet
- WebDataset training loaders must tolerate isolated corrupt samples by logging shard/key/member context and skipping the failed sample, while prediction and evaluation loaders remain strict unless explicitly configured otherwise.
- Large SenseVoiceRWKV runs may use ZeRO-1 with optimizer offload disabled and gradient checkpointing disabled when GPU memory leaves enough headroom; this is an optimization-only change and must not alter checkpoint resume semantics or the data-skip counter.
- Clean-data CTC curriculum stages must filter by CTC alignability under the actual training tokenizer and frontend downsampling before bucketed training. For the AuRWKV/Qwen3-ASR + Omni CTC setup, keep only samples where `target_tokens + adjacent_repeated_target_tokens <= aut_conv2d8_out_lengths(num_frames)`; for SenseVoice LFR, use `ceil(num_frames / 6)`. The earlier `easy_clean_librispeech_aishell3` stage was clean but not CTC-aligned; English LibriSpeech samples were often too token-dense after 8x downsampling and produced `zero_infinity=True` loss values of `0.0000`.
- CTC-aligned clean stages should be rebuilt with `text_normalization: ctc`, unknown-token filtering enabled, and a configurable positive `min_logit_required_ratio`. A ratio of exactly `1.0` only proves mathematical CTC feasibility; it may still be too tight for English character-level labels because it leaves almost no blank / repeat alignment slack.
- 2026-06-09: Stage6 CTC-draft AR continuation uses an offline first-pass cache generated from a fixed CTC checkpoint. The cache is a JSONL map keyed by utterance id and stores the normalized CTC hypothesis as `{ctc_draft}` for the decoder prompt. CTC labels remain clean and punctuation-stripped; AR targets keep runtime text normalization and language-confirmation/chat-template behavior. For draft-conditioned AR training, prefer filtering the WebDataset length index to cached utterances so most training samples have a draft instead of silently falling back to an empty prompt field.
- 2026-06-09: Add a Best-RQ probe before committing to a full encoder pre-training stage. The first slice is `SenseVoiceRWKVEncoder + fixed random-projection quantizer + Best-RQ prediction head`, initialized from the current CTC checkpoint and trained on audio-only WebDataset batches. The Best-RQ head and quantizer are discarded after pre-training; the resulting checkpoint is used only as an encoder initialization for a fresh CTC curriculum run. This should be evaluated by early CTC WER/CER and convergence speed before changing the main CTC-draft pipeline.
- 2026-06-09: The accepted Best-RQ continuation pipeline is: wait for a stable latest CTC/joint checkpoint, run a longer Best-RQ encoder pretrain from that checkpoint, initialize a CTC-only curriculum from the Best-RQ checkpoint, then generate an offline CTC draft cache from the CTC curriculum checkpoint. The CTC stage must keep `decoder_enabled: false`, clean CTC text normalization, and SenseVoice multilingual tokenizer labels; AR/draft-conditioned training happens only after this CTC cache is generated.
- 2026-06-09 recovery note: the Best-RQ -> CTC -> draft pipeline must support a `draft-only` resume path from an already completed CTC run. This is needed when the tmux-launched CTC process starts after the monitor's first process check and the outer pipeline exits before draft generation, even though the CTC training later completes successfully. The recovery path should reuse the generated CTC config, `model_config.yaml`, and latest stable `step-*.pt` without rerunning Best-RQ or CTC.
- 2026-06-09 full-draft note: the first offline draft cache used quota sampling and produced about half of the `stage6c_clean50_hard50` training rows. For draft-conditioned AR training on this stage, prefer a full cache built by generating only utterances missing from the sampled base cache, then merging by `utt_id` and filtering the WebDataset length index to the merged cache ids.
- 2026-06-09 full-draft parallelism note: CTC draft expansion can run as multiple independent prediction processes, but each process must own an isolated physical GPU via per-process `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<gpu_id>` and use `--device cuda:0` inside that restricted view. Do not launch several predictors with a shared visible CUDA device set and different `--device cuda:N`, because the current RWKV CUDA inference path is not stable under that multi-process configuration.
	- 2026-06-09 stage6d draft-conditioned AR run: start joint CTC+AR training from the Best-RQ-initialized CTC `step-5000.pt`, using the full offline CTC draft cache (`128,116` draft records and `130,357` cached length-index rows) as an AR prompt hint. Keep CTC labels punctuation-stripped with SenseVoice tokenizer; keep AR targets in runtime-normalized RWKV decoder text with language confirmation/chat-template behavior. Sidecar evaluation for this run must use sampling and pass the same draft cache into AR prediction so AR metrics reflect the training-time conditioning path.
	- Draft-conditioned AR must remain audio-conditioned, not draft-only. The decoder input context should include the projected continuous encoder acoustic hiddens together with the CTC-selected draft tokens rendered in the prompt, so the AR decoder can use both the discrete first-pass transcript and the higher-information acoustic representation.
	- 2026-06-09 stage6d eval guard note: the full-draft length index is intentionally filtered to cached training utterances and currently contains no `eval` split rows. Training-loop step/epoch loss eval on that index must therefore skip empty eval loaders instead of recording `nan` or ranking step checkpoints by an empty metric. For this stage, checkpoint selection should use sidecar normalized WER/CER; in-loop step eval can be disabled while the draft-conditioned run is active.
	- 2026-06-09 sidecar note: when a run's training config points to a train-only filtered length index, sidecar prediction must not pass that length index to eval-split prediction commands. It should fall back to raw WebDataset shard iteration so eval split samples can still be selected from the root `webdataset_index.json`; AR sidecar evaluation should still use the just-generated CTC prediction JSONL as the draft cache for the same samples.
	- 2026-06-10 metric note: AR language confirmation remains part of the decoder target format, but it is not speech content. Sidecar and validation WER/CER must strip the generated confirmation prefix before ASR tokenization so short utterances such as `六` or `for example` are not penalized by the fixed metadata phrase.
		- 2026-06-10 large CTC continuation: before returning to AR correction, train a larger CTC-only stage from the best recent CTC checkpoint. The stage must keep `decoder_enabled=false`, `decoder_loss_weight=0`, `ctc_loss_weight=1`, `text_normalization=ctc`, `tokenizer_append_eos=false`, SenseVoice multilingual CTC tokenizer labels, and sidecar/selector WER-CER checkpoint selection. CTC normalization must remove non-pronounced markup such as punctuation tags and bracketed noise/laughter/music/silence annotations so forced CTC alignment is not asked to emit labels for unspoken metadata.
		- 2026-06-11 post-stage7 draft-conditioned AR plan: after the large CTC-only stage finishes, select the initialization checkpoint by normalized WER/CER over an existing-checkpoint candidate set, not by eval loss alone and not from checkpoint files already removed by top-k cleanup. Generate an offline CTC draft cache for the full stage7 train split with one isolated prediction process per physical GPU, merge by utterance id, and filter the length index to cached utterances before CTC+AR training. The joint run must keep CTC labels in `text_normalization=ctc` with no EOS while AR targets use `decoder_text_normalization=runtime`, language-confirmation target prefixes, chat-style language prompts, and the CTC draft prompt together with continuous acoustic encoder hiddens.
		- Full offline draft generation may use a fast CTC greedy-collapse path (`beam_size<=1`) for throughput when producing large training caches. This does not change checkpoint selection or public CTC evaluation, which should continue to use the configured CTC beam settings; generated draft cache metadata/logs must make the decode strategy visible so AR experiments can distinguish greedy-draft and beam-draft inputs.
		- Full offline draft generation must stream JSONL predictions to disk while decoding instead of buffering an entire shard group in memory. Long shard groups can take hours; partial output should be visible for progress inspection and should survive predictor crashes so the run can be restarted or repaired without losing all completed utterances.
		- Parallel draft generation must avoid assigning multiple active predictor processes to the same physical GPU while leaving another configured GPU idle. If the scheduler cannot dynamically track the freed GPU, use one static worker queue per GPU rather than round-robin assignment by launch count.
		- The merged draft cache consumed by training must be compact. Keep raw per-part prediction JSONL files for debugging, but the merged training cache should contain only `utt_id`, normalized `pred_text` / `ctc_draft`, `draft_source_part`, and decode metadata. Do not carry alignments, token ids, or debug payloads into the train-time cache because every training rank needs to load the draft map.
		- Stage7 draft-conditioned CTC+AR restart scripts must prefer `resume_from: latest` when the AR run directory already has a complete `latest_checkpoint.yaml` and DeepSpeed checkpoint. This preserves optimizer state and `epoch_batch_offset`, so a crash/restart does not reread the already-trained prefix of the filtered length index.
		- 2026-06-12 Stage7 OOM recovery: a draft-conditioned AR restart hit CUDA OOM at step 3642 inside decoder cross entropy with batch size 4 and padded decoder text length 1292. The restart script should cap `decoder_text_token_budget` more conservatively by default and set `PYTORCH_ALLOC_CONF=expandable_segments:True` before torch initialization. This is a targeted AR-memory guard; it does not change CTC label normalization, draft conditioning, or checkpoint resume semantics.

Before generating another AR draft cache, continue with a CTC-only full-mix stage from the latest Stage7 checkpoint so the acoustic model sees substantially more hard English/Chinese speech. 		- 2026-06-13 CTC label normalization update: CTC targets should represent pronounced content only. AR targets remain `runtime` normalized so punctuation and richer text formatting stay in the decoder objective rather than the CTC alignment objective.

Use this sampled index through `DRAFT_SOURCE_LENGTH_INDEX_PATH` for the next offline CTC draft cache; do not start full 22.9M-row draft generation unless this smaller balanced AR stage shows clear value.

For sampled balanced length indexes, prefer an index-partition mode that writes one mixed length-index part per physical GPU and runs prediction with `--webdataset-shard-pattern "*.tar"`. This keeps coverage exact and avoids brittle shard-range pattern maintenance.
		- 2026-06-15 draft-generation prepare-only verification: `DRAFT_PARTITION_MODE=round_robin_index DRAFT_INDEX_PARTS=4 DRAFT_PREPARE_ONLY=1` split the Stage10b balanced2M sampled index into `length_parts/part_00.jsonl` ... `part_03.jsonl`, exactly `500,000` rows each. Keep the batch16 run active unless it OOMs, then fall back to batch8 or batch12.
		- Long draft-cache generation must be resumable at part granularity. If a part JSONL exists without its `.done` marker, a restart should not delete it by default; instead it should build a temporary length index excluding already emitted `utt_id`s and append only missing predictions to the same part file. This keeps multi-hour draft jobs from losing progress after a process crash or host interruption.
		- Draft-cache length-index partitioning should preserve tar locality. A sampled index in random row order causes each GPU worker to jump across many `.tar` shards, leaving GPUs mostly idle while loader workers wait on random disk reads. For large draft generation, prefer a `shard_round_robin_index` partition mode that groups rows by `shard_name`, sorts each shard by member offset/key, and assigns whole shards to GPU parts with approximately balanced row counts. This should reduce redundant tar opens and random seeks compared with row-level round-robin partitioning.
		- 2026-06-15 planned Stage11 draft-conditioned CTC+AR: when the balanced2M draft cache finishes and `webdataset_lengths.cached.jsonl` is filtered to cached utterances, initialize from Stage10b `wercer_best.pt` and train a joint CTC+AR stage on the balanced draft subset. Use a conservative first run (`ctc_loss_weight=0.3`, `decoder_loss_weight=0.7`, LR around `1e-5`, micro-batch `4`, grad-accum `4`, decoder text token budget `1024`) and select later checkpoints by normalized sidecar WER/CER rather than training loss alone.

Pure length bucketing can therefore produce long runs of source-skewed batches even when the input JSONL order is shuffled. Extend `bucket_source_interleave` to the length-index sampler as well as bucket-manifest loading: when enabled, build length-bucketed batches within each source label and round-robin source batch groups before the usual per-epoch global batch shuffle. This keeps frame efficiency while reducing English/Chinese source skew during Stage11.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage11 direction policy: this draft-conditioned AR continuation must not use direction dropout. Keep encoder training/eval in full bidirectional mode by forcing `direction_variant=none`, `p_start=0.0`, `p_max=0.0`, `warmup_steps=0`, and `ramp_steps=0` in the generated Stage11 config. `direction_variant=none` must return full forward/backward masks even if a probability field is accidentally changed later. This does not disable ordinary module dropout or draft-prompt dropout.
			- 2026-06-15 direction-dropout default guard: while the long-term dual-mode design can revisit direction dropout as a separate ablation, the current CTC/CTC+AR stabilization stages must not enable it accidentally. Direction-dropout scheduler, synthetic training, and training config dataclass defaults should therefore be `direction_variant=none`, `p_start=0.0`, `p_max=0.0`, `warmup_steps=0`, and `ramp_steps=0`; any experiment that re-enables direction dropout must do so explicitly in `Spec.md` and in the run config.
			- 2026-06-15 direction-dropout hard guard: the active training code must reject non-disabled direction dropout at runtime for the current stabilization line, because stale generated configs still contain historical `direction_variant=drop_both` values. This guard applies only to encoder direction masking. It must not be confused with ordinary model parameter dropout or decoder CTC-draft prompt dropout, which may still be used as regularization/data augmentation.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage11 draft-noise guard: early balanced2M draft inspection shows usable near-transcripts but also a material wrong-language failure mode in GigaSpeech rows. A live 2k-row front-of-cache sanity sample measured about `37.57%` WER / `23.34%` CER on early English rows and included English references decoded as Chinese text. Draft-conditioned AR training should therefore support deterministic CTC draft dropout in the decoder prompt. Start Stage11 with random draft dropout around `0.20`, and drop clearly wrong-language drafts with probability `1.00` so AR does not condition on high-confidence nonsensical cross-language hints. This changes only the prompt hint; CTC labels remain clean, AR targets still include language confirmation/runtime text, and continuous encoder acoustic hiddens remain in the decoder context.
			- 2026-06-15 live Stage11 draft-quality sample: at `1,201,152` generated draft rows, a deterministic 20k-row reservoir sample (`seed=20260615`, metric normalization `ctc`) measured corpus `37.61%` WER / `23.67%` CER overall, `36.55%` / `22.87%` on GigaSpeech English, and `43.62%` / `43.64%` on WenetSpeech Chinese. Empty normalized drafts were rare (`0.07%`), but English wrong-language drafts remained material (`21.61%` of sampled English refs had CJK in the draft). Keep `decoder_ctc_draft_language_mismatch_dropout_prob=1.00`; keep the random draft dropout at `0.20` for the first Stage11 run so the decoder still sees useful near-transcripts while learning to recover from missing/noisy hints.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage11 mixed-script draft guard: wrong-language detection must not rely only on majority script. Some early GigaSpeech drafts are mixed strings such as CJK tokens plus English words; these can be majority-Latin but still impossible for an English reference transcript. For English reference text, any CJK character in the CTC draft should be treated as a language mismatch. For Chinese reference text, a Latin-only draft should be treated as a mismatch. This guard only blanks the draft prompt according to `decoder_ctc_draft_language_mismatch_dropout_prob`; it must not remove the training sample or change CTC / AR targets.
				- 2026-06-15 Stage11 sidecar reproducibility guard: because AR preview uses sampling rather than greedy/beam decode, sidecar checkpoint selection must pass and record a fixed sampling seed. Use `SIDECAR_AR_SEED=20260615` unless an ablation explicitly changes the decode policy; otherwise normalized WER/CER comparisons between checkpoints include decode-time variance.
				- 2026-06-15 Stage11 restart guard: the balanced2M draft generation and draft-conditioned CTC+AR run are long enough that a silent training exit should be handled automatically. Run a separate guard session that waits for the final draft cache and filtered length index, then restarts Stage11 through the existing start script with `resume_from: auto` only when no matching training process is active and the latest exported step checkpoint is below `max_steps`. The Stage11 start script itself must own a run-dir lock and re-check for an active matching training process so the after-draft handoff and restart guard cannot race into duplicate training commands. This preserves DeepSpeed `latest_checkpoint.yaml` / `epoch_batch_offset` resume semantics and must not restart a normally completed run.
				- 2026-06-15 Stage11 process-detection fix: training liveness checks must match only actual training launchers or trainer children for the generated config, not arbitrary shell, grep, sidecar, or monitor commands that merely contain the config path. A false positive here can skip the only training start while sidecar waits forever for `train_config.yaml`. Use command-name and argument filtering in both the handoff start script and the restart guard.
				- 2026-06-15 Stage11 cached-subset loader fix: when a run explicitly provides `webdataset_length_index_path` for a sampled/draft-cached subset and leaves `webdataset_bucket_manifest_path` unset, the loader must prefer that explicit length index and must not auto-discover the root dataset's default bucket manifest. The default bucket manifest was built for the full GigaSpeech/WenetSpeech tar set and bypasses the cached 2M filter, causing repeated `Missing CTC draft` decode skips and inaccurate data-progress semantics. Only use a bucket manifest in this situation when the config explicitly names one that was built for the same cached subset.
		- 2026-06-15 Stage11 prediction guard: prediction / sidecar / public benchmark should not inherit training-time random draft dropout by default because it is a robustness regularizer, not the best inference policy. They should, however, be able to apply the deterministic wrong-language draft guard from the checkpoint tokenizer config so AR is not conditioned on a first-pass transcript in the wrong language. Evaluation commands may still override random draft dropout explicitly for ablations, but the default prediction path keeps random draft dropout at `0.0` and uses the saved mismatch-dropout setting.
		- 2026-06-15 Stage11 metric-normalization guard: sidecar and public benchmark scripts must keep prediction-time text normalization separate from metric-time normalization. Prediction/ref rendering can stay `runtime` so AR punctuation and readable previews are preserved, but WER/CER should default to the stricter ASR-content metric normalization equivalent to the CTC label normalizer: strip non-pronounced punctuation/symbols/non-speech annotations, remove project-generated language-confirmation prefixes, case-fold, and verbalize Arabic numerals into language-appropriate spoken forms before WER/CER tokenization. This value must be wired explicitly as `METRIC_NORMALIZATION=ctc` for Stage11 sidecar rather than relying on helper defaults. This avoids penalizing acceptable pairs such as `21` vs `twenty one` or `2024年` vs `二零二四年` while keeping raw pred/ref JSONL available for later rescoring.
		- 2026-06-15 Stage11 sidecar length-diagnostic fix: preview `pred/ref` length ratios and short/long comments must use the same normalized ASR-content text as WER/CER, not raw decoder token counts. Raw AR token counts include required language-confirmation metadata such as `This is English text.` / `这是中文文字。`, so using them for length-bias diagnosis overstates AR over-generation and can make otherwise correct punctuation/language-format outputs look long.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage11 sampling fix: AR sidecar evaluation must use standard nucleus sampling semantics. Top-p filtering should keep the smallest sorted token set whose cumulative probability reaches or exceeds `top_p`, always keeping at least one token. An implementation that keeps only tokens with cumulative probability `<= top_p` drops the threshold-crossing token and makes conservative ASR sampling narrower than intended, sometimes making the sidecar look closer to greedy than the configured sampling policy.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage11 early stop decision: the first balanced2M draft-conditioned CTC+AR run (`ctc_loss_weight=0.3`, `decoder_loss_weight=0.7`, `decoder_ctc_draft_dropout_prob=0.20`, LR `1e-5`) improved the CTC branch on the fixed 64-sample hard preview (`step-2500` CTC `36.97% / 23.98%`) but the AR branch regressed and showed free paraphrase / repetition failure modes (`step-3000` AR `50.71% / 34.89%`, WenetSpeech AR `67.50% / 67.50%`). Stop this run early rather than letting a decoder-heavy objective degrade the correction behavior.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage11b correction restart: restart from the Stage10b CTC `wercer_best.pt`, not from the unstable Stage11 joint checkpoint, and use a more conservative correction recipe: keep `direction_variant=none`, keep the same balanced2M draft cache and source-interleaved length index, set `ctc_loss_weight=0.8`, `decoder_loss_weight=0.2`, lower LR to about `5e-6`, and set random `decoder_ctc_draft_dropout_prob=0.0` while retaining wrong-language draft dropout at `1.0`. The intent is to make AR learn small draft/audio corrections without overpowering the CTC acoustic objective or learning to hallucinate around missing draft hints.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage11b early-stop decision: the conservative restart still failed as a direct final ASR decoder. On the fixed 64-sample hard sidecar, `step-500` measured CTC `38.81% / 24.70%` vs AR sampling `53.12% / 35.03%`; `step-1000` worsened to CTC `39.24% / 24.51%` vs AR sampling `59.35% / 38.64%`, with WenetSpeech AR `76.25% / 87.50%`. Typical failures are repeated phrases (`不不不...`, `she's in the sleep...`) and fluent semantic rewrites. Stop Stage11b at `step-1000`; more of this objective is not a good use of compute.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage11c decode-safety requirement: draft-conditioned AR sampling must not be allowed to replace a stronger CTC transcript with an unconstrained hallucination. Add an optional prediction-time CTC-draft fallback guard, disabled by default for ablations and enabled for Stage11c sidecar/final-output evaluation. The guard still runs sampling, but if the sampled ASR-content text diverges too far from the same-sample CTC draft by normalized CER, has an abnormal normalized length ratio, or contains obvious repeated spans, the reported final `pred_text` falls back to the CTC draft and records the fallback reason in debug metadata. This is a safety floor, not a claim that AR has improved; a successful Stage11c still needs AR to beat or safely improve CTC on normalized WER/CER.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage11c training restart: restart again from Stage10b `wercer_best.pt`, freeze the encoder and CTC head, and train only the RWKV decoder plus encoder-to-decoder projection on the balanced2M draft-conditioned subset. This keeps the CTC acoustic baseline fixed while testing whether the decoder can learn a correction/scoring role. Use full bidirectional masks (`direction_variant=none`) and keep CTC labels clean even if `ctc_loss_weight` is reduced to zero for this decoder-only stage.
			- 2026-06-15 CTC n-best rescoring fix: when using RWKV decoder sequence scoring to rerank CTC beam candidates, pass the same per-sample `decoder_prompt_before_audio` / CTC-draft prompt used by Stage11 training. Scoring CTC candidates without the draft prompt is off-policy for the draft-conditioned decoder and should not be used to judge whether CTC-constrained AR can help.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage11c sidecar draft-cache fix: sidecar CTC preview must not inherit the train-set `decoder_ctc_draft_cache_path` or `decoder_ctc_draft_missing_policy=error` from the training config, because public/eval preview samples are not guaranteed to exist in the train draft cache. CTC preview should run without a decoder draft cache, then draft-conditioned AR preview should use the just-generated same-checkpoint CTC JSONL as its draft source.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage11c early-stop decision: the decoder-only frozen-encoder run is not improving the ASR objective. On the fixed 64-sample hard sidecar, `step-500` measured CTC/fallback-final AR `38.53% / 25.04%` WER/CER while raw AR was worse at `45.75% / 32.24%`; `step-1000` regressed further to fallback-final AR `38.67% / 25.18%` and raw AR `49.01% / 33.01%`. The fallback guard prevented most hallucinations but also masked the fact that direct sampled AR generation is not yet a reliable correction model. Stop Stage11c at `step-1000` and do not spend the remaining 4k steps on this objective.
			- 2026-06-15 Stage12 CTC recovery plan: return compute to the CTC acoustic path. Start with a short clean/wide replay from Stage10b `wercer_best.pt` on the complete `clean_ctc_voxbox_webdataset` mix (LibriSpeech, AISHELL3, CommonVoice EN/CN) using clean CTC labels, no decoder, no direction dropout, and normalized WER/CER sidecar. The immediate goal is to verify that the current encoder/tokenizer can still improve on clean public-style data before resuming harder GigaSpeech/WenetSpeech fullmix training. Any CTC start script used for Stage12 must explicitly force `direction_variant=none`, `p_start=0.0`, `p_max=0.0`, `warmup_steps=0`, and `ramp_steps=0`.

The generated config sets `direction_variant=none`, `p_start=0.0`, `p_max=0.0`, `warmup_steps=0`, and `ramp_steps=0`, and the training log confirms full bidirectional encoder masks. Do not describe ordinary parameter dropout or SpecAugment as direction dropout in this stabilization line.

The Stage10b baseline is `30.20% / 20.44%` WER/CER, exact `6/64`. Stage12a improved steadily after step3000: `step-4000` `29.74% / 20.19%`, `step-5000` `29.19% / 19.75%`, and final `step-6000` `28.45% / 18.95%`, exact `8/64`. The final `step-6000` checkpoint is recorded as Stage12a `wercer_best.pt`. This confirms that the current encoder/tokenizer still benefits from clean/wide CTC replay with light SpecAugment and no direction dropout, but the absolute error rates remain far from public-paper targets. Next run a full public CTC benchmark from Stage12a `wercer_best.pt` before deciding whether to resume harder GigaSpeech/WenetSpeech CTC, continue clean/public-style replay, or generate a new CTC draft cache for CTC+AR.

The cleanwide replay distribution explains the language imbalance: `commonvoice_en` is `77.77%` of samples and `64.88%` of frames, `librispeech` is `15.52%` / `30.63%`, while Chinese is only `aishell3` `4.42%` / `2.59%` plus `commonvoice_cn` `2.29%` / `1.89%`. The implementation applies the bias before `log_softmax` by adding it only to the blank logit (`negative` values penalize blank), exposes it in labeled and unlabeled CTC prediction CLIs, and passed targeted tests plus `py_compile`. Stop blank-bias decode repair; the next attempt should combine the CV-improving replay with Wenet anchors in one training stage rather than isolated Wenet-only repair.

First run `N=5`, start from Stage180k `step192`, reload Nano-tail bridge plus Nano CTC decoder/head from ModelScope, freeze BiRWKV, and train Nano-tail bridge + CTC decoder/head on Stage179a easy online teacher data with bounded nonblank/window/sequence objectives and small hidden alignment. Gate by whether this teacher-initialized assistant recovers nonempty Nano-like CTC emissions beyond Stage195/199 without over-emission; if it still collapses to blank, the bridge needs a deeper teacher-state initialization or a larger multi-layer state distillation phase before CTC logits.
					- 2026-07-01 Stage200 Nano-tail teacher-assistant result and Stage201 plan: implemented `ctc_bridge_type=nano_encoder_tailN` with direct loading of the last Nano `tp_encoders` plus `tp_norm`; tests and a smoke load confirmed `67/67` bridge tensors loaded, alongside the Nano CTC decoder (`84`) and remapped head (`60,515` rows). Stage200 `N=5` started from Stage180k `step192`, froze BiRWKV, and trained the Nano-tail bridge plus Nano CTC decoder/head (`54.86M` trainable parameters) for `64` easy-split online steps. The assistant reduced selected hidden MSE, but the CTC path became fully blank: heldout Stage200 `step32` and `step64` both measured Nano WER/CER/token ER `100.000/100.000/100.000`, exact `0%`, length ratio `0.000000`, with average blank probability still around `0.995`. This means the teacher-tail blocks are preserving the Nano blank/background manifold but are not creating emission peaks from the off-manifold BiRWKV states. Stage201 should restart from the clean Stage180k `step192` + ModelScope Nano-tail/decoder/head initialization, not from the blank-collapsed Stage200 checkpoint, and run a short nonblank-emission warmup: apply a negative blank-bias delta at init, disable hidden/all-frame/full-KL/blank/mass losses, freeze the BiRWKV encoder, and train only Nano-tail bridge + CTC decoder/head with high teacher-token presence plus teacher nonblank-window top-k/margin objectives. Gate success by nonempty prediction count and length ratio recovering above Stage187/199 while keeping token ER from exploding beyond Stage195; only after emissions exist should full-vocabulary KL be reintroduced.
					- 2026-07-01 Stage201 Nano-tail5 nonblank warmup result and Stage202 plan: Stage201 restarted from clean Stage180k `step192`, loaded Nano-tail5/decoder/head from ModelScope, applied CTC head blank-bias delta `-3.0`, froze BiRWKV, and trained Nano-tail5 + CTC decoder/head for `64` easy-split online steps with only sequence-presence and nonblank-window/top-k/margin objectives. Training was healthy and reduced average blank probability, but the raw greedy CTC remained almost all blank: heldout `step16/32/48` had `0/200` nonempty predictions and length ratio `0.000`; `step64` had only `3/200` nonempty predictions, `5` predicted tokens total, length ratio `0.0085`, and Nano WER/CER/token ER `100.000/100.106/100.000`. A decode-time blank-bias sweep on `step64` showed that additional blank penalties can force emissions but do not solve token identity: `blank -2/-4/-6/-8` gave length ratios `0.103/0.402/0.494/0.499` with token ER `100.348/101.974/104.530/104.646`. Therefore the problem is not just a blank threshold; Nano-tail5 is still not mapping BiRWKV states into the correct Nano token manifold. Stage202 should test a shallower teacher-assistant bridge, `ctc_bridge_type=nano_encoder_tail1`, starting again from Stage180k `step192` with ModelScope Nano-tail1/decoder/head, blank-bias delta `-4.0`, frozen BiRWKV, and a sequence-stabilized objective from the start: small Nano greedy-token CTC sequence loss, sequence-presence, local nonblank-window/top-k/margin, and tiny blank/mass calibration. Gate by length ratio entering the `0.15-0.6` band and token ER improving below Stage195/187 rather than merely deleting or over-inserting emissions.
					- 2026-07-01 Stage202 Nano-tail1 sequence result and Stage203 plan: Stage202 loaded Nano-tail1/decoder/head from ModelScope (`15` bridge tensors), applied blank-bias delta `-4.0`, froze BiRWKV, and trained `64` easy-split online steps with sequence CTC, presence, nonblank-window/top-k/margin, and tiny blank/mass calibration. Tail1 is easier to emit than tail5, but still not aligned: heldout `step16/32/48/64` measured length ratios `0.0135/0.0268/0.0674/0.1136`, nonempty counts `6/9/25/36` out of `200`, and token ER `100.000/100.116/100.348/101.045`. The predictions contain many non-pronunciation or invalid units such as `<|nospeech|>`, timestamp/special ids, pure whitespace, replacement-character byte fragments, and punctuation-only tokens; these are not valid CTC acoustic labels and directly compete with teacher text tokens. Stage203 should add a tokenizer-derived CTC suppression mask before any further loss tuning: when using `sensevoice_tiktoken`, build a project-token mask that suppresses special/timestamp/control ids, pure whitespace tokens, single-token decodes that normalize to empty under CTC normalization, and single-token decodes containing Unicode replacement characters, while preserving the project blank id. Apply this mask at the CTC-logit level for training losses and prediction, not as a text-only postprocess. This is an output-space constraint aligned with CTC label cleanup; it does not alter the BiRWKV/RWKV-7 encoder architecture. After tests, first run an inference-only diagnostic on Stage202 `step64` with suppression enabled, then train Stage203 from Stage202 `step64` or clean Stage180k depending on whether suppression reveals useful hidden token probabilities.
					- 2026-07-01 Stage203 suppression diagnostic and training plan: implemented tokenizer-derived `ctc_suppressed_token_ids` and model-level CTC logit masking; tests verify SenseVoice `<|nospeech|>`, timestamp/special ids, pure whitespace, replacement-character byte fragments, and invalid single-token decodes are suppressed while normal text tokens and blank are preserved. The real FunASR-Nano tokenizer yields `3,629` suppressed token ids out of `60,515`. An inference-only diagnostic on Stage202 `step64` with suppression enabled reduced invalid-token competition but also exposed that valid text-token logits are still weak: token ER improved from `101.045` to `100.116`, while length ratio fell from `0.1136` to `0.0248`. Therefore Stage203 should continue from Stage202 `step64` rather than restart from clean Nano init, enable `ctc_suppress_non_pronunciation_tokens: true` during training, keep BiRWKV frozen, keep Nano-tail1/decoder/head trainable, and increase valid nonblank pressure modestly (`sequence=2`, `presence=2`, `nonblank_window=4`, `margin=1`, `topk=1`, blank/mass `0.02`) without full-KL. Gate by increasing suppressed-decoded length ratio above `0.05` while keeping token ER at or below the Stage202 suppressed diagnostic; if it still collapses to blank, the next step should add a valid-token class prior or train a smaller bridge/head without the Nano CTC head bias rather than continuing tail calibration.
					- 2026-06-15 public benchmark target context: current open ASR reports are far below the active hard-set CTC error rates. Fun-ASR Technical Report Table 1 reports Fun-ASR-Nano around `AIShell1 CER 1.22`, `LibriSpeech clean/other WER 1.51/3.03`, and `WenetSpeech Net WER 5.46`; Qwen3-ASR reports Qwen3-ASR-0.6B around `LibriSpeech clean/other WER 2.11/4.55`, `GigaSpeech WER 8.88`, and `CV-en WER 9.92`, with Qwen3-ASR-1.7B lower on most of those. Treat these as public-reference targets for later full-set benchmark reporting, while recognizing that the project still needs large improvements before paper-level claims are meaningful.

### Phase 7: Dual-Mode Validation
- chunked `L2R` streaming runner
- `Bi` and `Alt` offline runners
  - benchmark and WER/CER scripts
- public full-set benchmark manifests for clean and non-clean ASR reporting:
  - `zh_clean`: AISHELL-1 test
  - `en_clean`: LibriSpeech test-clean and test-other
  - `zh_non_clean`: WenetSpeech TEST_NET
  - `en_non_clean`: Mozilla Common Voice English test
  - all benchmark reports must use the normalized ASR WER/CER metric described in the sidecar section
  - reports should retain raw pred/ref JSONL so CTC and RWKV decoder AR can be rescored after metric changes
  - benchmark prediction normalization and metric normalization must be separate knobs; default prediction normalization is `runtime`, while default metric normalization is `ctc` so punctuation, language-confirmation metadata, non-speech annotations, and Arabic numerals are normalized before WER/CER
  - long full-set prediction runs must emit periodic sample-count progress so tmux sessions can be distinguished from hangs while JSONL output is still buffered
  - AR sampling benchmark runs must record and fix the sampling seed because WER/CER otherwise has decode-time variance
  - draft-conditioned AR benchmark runs must generate CTC predictions on the same public eval split first and pass that eval prediction JSONL as the AR `{ctc_draft}` cache; do not reuse a train-set offline draft cache for public eval samples
- same-checkpoint mode-comparison reports
- standalone prediction CLI with JSONL outputs

Status:
- in progress
- greedy CTC decoding and same-checkpoint `Bi/L2R/Alt` token-level evaluation implemented
  - current validation still had token-error metrics on manifest token ids; replace with normalized-text WER/CER computation as the primary sidecar and validation report metric
- standalone prefix-beam prediction CLI and exported utterance-level hypotheses are the next slice

## 9. Immediate Next Steps

1. Finish `uv sync` so `pytest`, `ruff`, and runtime dependencies are available in `.venv`.
2. Implement the bidirectional TimeMixer wrapper and mode masks.
3. Implement fixed-rate Direction Dropout first; keep scheduler logic only for ablations.
4. Add optimizer parameter-group helpers that preserve upstream `att.w0` lr scaling and matrix-only weight decay.
5. Extend verification with runnable unit tests for:
   - forward shape
   - `v_first` propagation
   - direction masking
   - `Bi` vs chunked `L2R` mode semantics
   - chunk padding to fused-kernel constraints
   - causal chunk-by-chunk consistency in `L2R`

## 10. Open Questions

- Which dataset is first target: AISHELL, GigaSpeech, LibriSpeech, internal manifests, or mixed?
- Should we keep a byte-level tokenizer as an ablation against Whisper multilingual text-only CTC targets?
- What is the target `Bi -> L2R` WER delta that is acceptable for the first milestone?
- Should the fused CUDA kernel be mandatory for training, or optional behind a feature flag until the pure PyTorch path is verified?

## 11. Active Training

- 2026-07-19 Stage209 postmortem and Stage210 admission rules: normalized public-200 evaluation improved monotonically from Stage208, but remains far from the native Nano CTC target. Stage209 checkpoints at `5k/10k/15k/20k` reached LibriSpeech test-clean WER `32.230/30.915/30.106/29.702`, test-other WER `48.557/46.490/45.640/45.731`, and AISHELL-1 CER `44.395/41.431/40.545/39.966`; the corresponding Nano CTC references are `7.457/13.127/3.850`. Two implementation defects make the old logits-only data count misleading. First, Stage209 used the historical `sensevoice_lfr_fbank` frontend while Nano uses `WavFrontend`; a real LibriSpeech audit measured encoder-input RMSE `2.7904` and mean cosine `0.98270`, whereas the new `funasr_wav_frontend` path measured RMSE `0.1036` and cosine `0.999956`. Second, the DeepSpeed loop called `engine.zero_grad()` before every microbatch even with `gradient_accumulation_steps=4`, erasing the earlier microbatch gradients before each accumulation boundary. Remove that call and let `engine.step()` manage accumulation; Stage210 initially uses accumulation `1` so its data-batch and optimizer-step accounting are unambiguous.
- 2026-07-19 Stage210a raw-scale admission diagnostic: the first formal attempt was intentionally stopped before any checkpoint after 20 updates. Its 256-sample step-0 objective was `152.5995`, and the cyclic 40-47 layer group produced loss `1998.6116` while neighboring groups were mostly `3-21`. Because normalized MSE plus cosine is bounded near this scale, the outlier came from the provisional `raw_mse_weight=0.01` acting on not-yet-calibrated deep RWKV activations. Stage210a must therefore begin with normalized MSE plus cosine only (`raw_mse_weight=0`) to align representation direction/shape. Raw scale alignment may be introduced only after the fixed hidden objective stabilizes, with a separately admitted low weight; the stopped run is not a training result and consumed no checkpointed phase data.
- 2026-07-19 Stage210a stacked shape diagnostic: the run under `/tmp/rwkvasr_runs/sensevoice_rwkv_stage210_stage209_nanoqkv_exactfrontend_easy1490h_1ep_hidden_subblock_shape_lr1e5_4x4090`, W&B run `sxe1v4xc`, was stopped deliberately near step `370` before its first checkpoint after validating the stacked path. Its unshuffled 256-sample step-0 objective was `2.653313`; the first complete 70-layer cycle stayed within `2.5491-2.7505`, including layers 40-47 at `2.7366`, with all teacher records matched and maximum frame delta `0`. Peak reserved memory was `6.39 GiB`. This run is a diagnostic only and must not be treated as the admitted Stage210a result because accumulated upstream student error prevents it from isolating the temporal operator during initialization.
- 2026-07-19 Stage210a operator-local formal launch: the admitted teacher-forced run is active under `/tmp/rwkvasr_runs/sensevoice_rwkv_stage210_stage209_nanoqkv_exactfrontend_easy1490h_1ep_hidden_localshape_lr1e5_4x4090`, W&B run `12b5ex2r`. The bucket manifest resolves to exactly `30,064` data batches for one complete easy epoch with four GPUs, local batch `12`, frame budget `8,000`, and gradient accumulation `1`. Its unshuffled fixed 256-utterance step-0 objective is `2.377073`. The first 600 updates have complete teacher matching, zero missing records, zero frame delta, and stable finite layer losses; for the repeated layer 32-39 group, total loss changed from `2.2669` on its first sampled batch to `2.0935` at step 600, while temporal-mixer loss changed from `2.2309` to `2.0612`. This early training-batch comparison is directional evidence only; the fixed 256-utterance evaluations at `10k/20k/30k` remain the admission metric. - Stage210 hidden alignment is split into operator-local and stacked stages. The operator-local stage captures the exact Nano input to each encoder layer and applies the corresponding student `norm1 -> input_proj (layer 0 only) -> BiRWKV TimeMixer` to that teacher input. It must preserve BiRWKV forward/backward execution and RWKV-7 `v_first`: layer 0 is always evaluated as the shared `v_first` source even when it is not one of the loss layers. The local post-temporal residual and copied `norm2/feed_forward` then produce the student MLP target. This removes accumulated upstream student error from the temporal/MLP operator target and is the intended meaning of sub-block alignment. After local fixed metrics converge, the existing full student stack becomes the next hidden stage and aligns temporal, MLP, and complete block outputs under student-propagated inputs. Both modes use the same exact features, layer IDs, masks, and teacher tensors so their fixed losses are directly auditable; neither mode changes the BiRWKV architecture.
- Stage210 hidden objectives must report temporal/FFN/block student RMS, teacher RMS, their ratio, cosine, and a finite per-layer loss on the fixed evaluation set. Shape-only LayerNorm-MSE plus cosine is allowed for the initial operator-local stage, but it is not sufficient evidence for promotion because it is nearly invariant to activation magnitude. Unbounded raw MSE is prohibited for uncalibrated deep layers. Before stacked block or logits alignment, add and admit a bounded energy-normalized MSE term, `2 * mean((student-teacher)^2) / (mean(student^2) + mean(teacher^2) + eps)`, which preserves scale sensitivity without allowing a high-energy layer to dominate the batch. Promotion requires improving fixed-set shape and energy metrics without a pathological RMS ratio; do not infer scale convergence from training loss alone.
- 2026-07-19 Stage210 scale audit and objective correction: an independent nine-step four-GPU smoke on the exact formal initialization covered all 70 layers with finite losses/gradients, complete teacher matching, and zero frame delta. Mixer student/teacher RMS ratios by cyclic layer group were `2.49, 2.67, 1.89, 1.17, 1.24, 41.21, 0.38, 5.43, 1.95`; layers 40-47 had student/teacher RMS `459.12/11.14`. Mixer cosine remained near zero (`-0.021` to `0.042`) and bounded energy-normalized MSE remained near `2`. The Q/K/V transfer is therefore wired correctly but does not by itself calibrate the very different RWKV temporal operator. Shape-only metrics are invariant to common rescaling, while symmetric energy MSE has weak radial gradient when vectors are nearly orthogonal. Add a per-frame Smooth-L1 loss on `log(RMS(student)+eps) - log(RMS(teacher)+eps)` so angular shape and radial scale receive separate gradients; use provisional weights shape `1.0`, cosine `0.25`, energy `0.25`, and log-RMS `0.10`. Admit them only after another nine-step all-layer smoke remains finite, then stop the no-checkpoint shape-only run and restart Stage210a from the same Stage209 plus Nano initialization with a new fixed step-0 baseline and the original one-epoch/10k evaluation contract.
- Stage210 metric collection must not introduce a device synchronization per sample/layer/component event. Accumulate detached RMS, cosine, energy, and log-RMS sufficient statistics on the training device and transfer a stacked summary once per batch. The first scale-aware formal admission attempt established fixed step-0 loss `3.0252` and W&B run `j0k8141e`, but it may be restarted before any checkpoint solely to remove per-event `.item()` synchronization; the optimized replacement must reproduce the same fixed baseline within deterministic tolerance before it is admitted.
- 2026-07-19 Stage210a scale-aware pre-admission: the statistics-batched run under `/tmp/rwkvasr_runs/sensevoice_rwkv_stage210_stage209_nanoqkv_exactfrontend_easy1490h_1ep_hidden_localenergy_statsbatch_lr1e5_4x4090`, W&B run `hcw0y4yj`, reproduced fixed step-0 loss `3.0256` and reached step `820` with complete matching and zero frame delta before being stopped without a checkpoint to correct fixed-eval layer weighting. It also established that the hard layer 42-49 group was learning: mixer cosine reached `0.170`, RMS ratio fell from `18.9` to `14.5`, and group loss reached `2.706`. These are diagnostic training-batch results, not the promotion baseline.
- Stage210 fixed evaluation must use the same uniform cyclic layer sampler as training. Forcing boundary layers `0/49/50/69` into every one of the 64 fixed-eval batches gives those four layers half of all hidden events while each remaining layer is seen only about four times; such a loss is not a valid 70-layer promotion metric. Use eight uniformly cycled layers per eval batch so 256 utterances at eval batch size four cover every layer about seven to eight times. The `hcw0y4yj` run may be stopped before its first checkpoint solely to correct this evaluation bias; the replacement baseline supersedes `3.0256`, while the training objective, initialization, data order, and one-epoch contract stay unchanged.
- 2026-07-19 Stage210a exact-scale uniform-eval control: `/tmp/rwkvasr_runs/sensevoice_rwkv_stage210_stage209_nanoqkv_exactfrontend_easy1490h_1ep_hidden_localenergy_uniformeval_lr1e5_4x4090`, W&B run `a830xkj7`, established fixed unshuffled 256-utterance step-0 loss `3.0308`, complete teacher matching, zero frame delta, and finite all-layer execution. It was deliberately stopped without an admission checkpoint after step `4260` only after the paired same-data 350-step fixed evaluation proved that `rwkv_norm` converges substantially faster. This run is retained as an exact-scale control and must not be promoted; the replacement keeps the same data, objective, LR, checkpoint source, and `10k/20k/30k` gates while changing only QKV projection scale compatibility.
- 2026-07-19 Stage210a norm-matched formal admission: the replacement is active under `/tmp/rwkvasr_runs/sensevoice_rwkv_stage210_stage209_nanoqkv_rwkvnorm_exactfrontend_easy1490h_1ep_hidden_localenergy_uniformeval_lr1e5_4x4090`, W&B run `pvzk8t9u`. Its fixed unshuffled 256-utterance step-0 loss is `2.9998`, reproducing the independent ablation baseline `2.9997`; the first full 70-layer cycle has complete teacher matching, zero missing records, zero frame delta, and finite gradients. Device-resident FP16 teacher hidden targets are active, and first-step total/teacher time is `1.00/0.20 s`. This is the admitted Stage210a run: consume all `30,064` data batches, save and evaluate at `10k/20k/30k`, and do not stop early. The detached ten-minute monitor is `rwkvasr_stage210a_rwkvnorm_monitor` and writes `monitor_10min.log` under the run directory.
- Stage210 online hidden-state distillation must not round-trip same-rank teacher activations through host memory. Add an explicit device-resident layer-hidden record path for online hidden-only training: detach the requested Nano layer tensors, cast them to the same FP16 storage precision used by the existing CPU record path, and keep them on the teacher/student rank device until the batch loss is complete. The default record behavior stays CPU-backed for exporters and caches. The active uniform-eval run may reach its first `10k` checkpoint before adopting this optimization; resume from that exact DeepSpeed checkpoint so no samples or optimizer state are discarded, and compare first-step timing before/after. Device residency is a throughput change, not a promotion-metric change, so fixed loss must remain within normal FP16 numerical tolerance and all matching/frame-delta gates remain mandatory.
- The phase-2 full-logit path must provide the same explicit device-resident option for Nano's full CTC log-probability records. Keep FP16 storage and the existing blank/ignored-token projection unchanged, preserve CPU-backed records by default for export/cache use, and enable device residency only when teacher and student share the rank device. This removes a roughly `frames x 60,516` GPU-to-host-to-GPU transfer per batch without changing KL semantics.
- Device-resident full-logit distillation must keep time-map indices on the device of the tensor being indexed. The historical full-KL implementation forced nearest and linear-map indices to CPU because all cached targets were host-backed; applying that path to GPU-resident `full_log_probs` would reintroduce transfers and may fail with mixed-device `index_select`. Select full logits, top-k ids, and top-k probabilities with indices moved independently to each source tensor's device, and keep linear interpolation weights beside the full-logit tensor. Cover both nearest and linear maps with a CUDA-resident regression test before Stage210c is admitted.
- Stage210 fixed evaluation must also expose all-layer diagnostics instead of only a scalar objective. Aggregate each sampled layer's loss, energy-normalized MSE, log-RMS error, cosine, student RMS, teacher RMS, and RMS ratio over valid frames/elements and all four ranks; save the complete mapping beside each fixed-eval checkpoint and report per-layer loss/cosine/RMS ratio to W&B. Use these fixed-audio metrics, not variable training batches, to identify non-converging depth regions and to choose the hidden checkpoint promoted to stacked-block alignment.
- 2026-07-19 Stage210 distributed fixed-layer sampling correction: the first implementation selected layers from each rank's local `eval_batch_index`, so four ranks repeated the same 16 layer groups instead of using the intended 64 global groups. The scalar step-0/step-10k objective remains internally comparable because both evaluations use the same deterministic schedule, but its per-layer sample coverage is only one or two rank-synchronized groups and differs by up to `2x`; it is not the intended all-layer diagnostic. Derive the selector cursor from `eval_batch_index * world_size + rank`, which gives every layer seven or eight distinct global batch groups across the fixed 256-utterance evaluation. The Stage210 runner provides a zero-update `--eval-only` path and an explicit `--skip-nano-init` switch so reconstructed initialization and trained checkpoints can be scored without changing parameters. The corrected reconstructed baseline is `2.987220` with all 70 layers present and per-layer frame weights spanning `800-930`, versus `480-1000` under the repeated-rank schedule. Preserve the admitted process through its first 10k checkpoint, then score that checkpoint with the corrected schedule before making a Stage210b promotion decision; do not compare a corrected-schedule scalar directly with the historical local-schedule scalar.
- 2026-07-19 Stage210 real-audio hidden-hook semantic audit: a short Common Voice zh-CN utterance was run through the CPU Nano teacher while capturing layers `0/10/40/42/49/50/69`. Reconstructing Nano's residual formula from the captured `input/mixer/ffn` tensors matched captured block outputs with relative RMSE `0.000206-0.000657`. Loading all `564` matching Nano non-attention tensors into the student and recomputing its copied MLP on the exact teacher post-mixer inputs matched Nano FFN outputs with relative RMSE `0.000198-0.000521` and mean cosine `0.99999988-1.0`. This covers the main-to-TP normalization boundary and rules out wrong hook positions or FFN/Norm key mapping as the source of the remaining gap; the active error is in temporal-operator direction/scale alignment.
- 2026-07-19 Stage210 encoder-entry and fixed-input variance audit: the project and FunASR sinusoidal position encoders match exactly in FP32 (`max_abs=0`, relative error `0`); the actual BF16 student path versus the BF16-rounded FP32 teacher reference has relative error `0.00318` and cosine `0.999981`, which is expected precision loss rather than a different position formula. `funasr_wav_frontend` retains Nano's default `dither=1.0`, but two independent corrected-schedule reconstructions of the same 256-utterance step-0 model scored `2.987220` and `2.987181`, a delta of only `3.9e-5` (`0.0013%`). Keep the shared train-time frontend unchanged and treat this measured variance as fixed-eval numerical tolerance; it is far below a promotion-scale improvement and is not a candidate explanation for the distillation gap.
- Stage210 QKV warm-start must distinguish direction transfer from operator scale. A 70-layer ModelScope audit measured Nano Q/K/V weight-RMS ratios against the corresponding upstream RWKV-7 receptance/key/value initialization RMS: median `19.479/196.312/22.510`, with K as high as `288.085`. Exact transfer therefore drives RWKV's unnormalized `r * k * r_k * v` residual far outside its upstream operating scale even though the copied directions are useful. The paired four-GPU, 350-step, fixed-256 ablation used the same Stage209 checkpoint, data order, layer schedule, optimizer, and loss: exact QKV changed `3.0303 -> 2.9178` (`3.7%`), while `rwkv_norm` changed `2.9997 -> 2.2964` (`23.4%`) and finished `21.3%` lower. Stage210 therefore uses `rwkv_norm`: preserve every Nano Q/K/V matrix direction and relative structure, rescale only its global RMS to RWKV-7's upstream projection RMS (`0.5/sqrt(3C)` for receptance/value and `0.05/sqrt(3C)` for key), keep Nano output projection exact, and preserve all RWKV-only parameters. This is a scale-compatible Nano initialization, not random initialization or an architecture change.
- 2026-07-19 Stage210 formal-kernel parity audit: the active run uses upstream `rwkv7_clampw_v3` semantics, with decay `exp(-exp(-0.5) * sigmoid(raw_w))`, rather than the older wind-backstepping decay transform. A deterministic BF16 CUDA comparison against an independent FP32 recurrent reference measured forward relative L2 error `2.41e-6`; all six input-gradient cosines were `1.0`, the largest gradient relative L2 error was `1.55e-4`, and every gradient was finite. Preserve `cuda_clampw` for the admitted run and add a CUDA-gated forward/backward regression test. The kernel is not a candidate explanation for the remaining Nano hidden-state gap unless that regression fails on the target runtime.

Expose a guarded `1-70` layer-sample-count runner option and admit a 24-layer smoke only after it proves complete teacher matching, zero frame delta, finite gradients, and acceptable four-card memory. Build a zero-update control from the exact upstream TimeMixer initialization, then apply the same admitted Nano `rwkv_norm` Q/K/V/output transfer, first-layer SVD projection, copied Norm/MLP, and copied CTC decoder/head used by the active run. - Stage210 is an adaptive three-stage alignment, while the model architecture remains fixed. Stage210a is operator-local teacher-forced alignment of Nano SANM temporal outputs and the resulting copied-MLP branch outputs; Nano FFN/Norm parameters are copied exactly and frozen, so the MLP objective is a functional constraint on the TimeMixer result rather than a separate parameter-training stage. Stage210b starts only from a passing Stage210a checkpoint, propagates student states through the complete encoder, and aligns temporal, MLP, and full residual-block hidden states. Stage210c aligns the final full-vocabulary Nano CTC log probabilities through the copied five-layer Nano CTC decoder/head while retaining a decaying hidden-state anchor. A separate MLP-only stage is introduced only if the exact-copy semantic audit fails; the current real-audio audit passed. Promotion and loss reweighting are decided from fixed-set per-layer loss/cosine/RMS metrics and normalized WER/CER, never from variable training batches alone.
- Stage210c is split into two logits curricula, with launch weights treated as provisional until the Stage210b fixed gate. The `nonblank` admission phase uses exact Nano features, device-resident full logits, full-vocabulary KL on teacher nonblank frames plus their immediate neighbors, explicit blank/mass calibration, and a stacked temporal/MLP/block hidden anchor on one complete easy epoch. The copied Nano five-layer CTC decoder and CTC head remain frozen so this phase must move the encoder onto the Nano output manifold instead of destroying an already correct output path. Only after normalized English WER, Chinese CER, decoded-length ratio, fixed full-KL, and hidden metrics improve without blank collapse may the `full` phase use all-frame KL over the complete deduplicated easy/medium/hard/long pool for three epochs. With local batch `12`, four ranks, and an `8,000` frame budget, the manifests resolve to `30,064` nonblank-admission steps and `6,257,160` cumulative full-curriculum steps. The full phase reduces but does not initially remove the hidden anchor. Sequence, presence, margin, and window pseudo-label objectives remain disabled in both phases; if full-KL plateaus, change one loss family at a time based on the fixed gates.
- Stage210c fixed evaluation must decompose the logits objective rather than report only the weighted training sum. On the same unshuffled fixed audio, aggregate across all ranks: selected-frame full-vocabulary KL, selected and all-frame top-1 agreement, blank-probability MAE, teacher/student nonblank-frame rates, CTC-collapsed token edit rate, and student/teacher collapsed-length ratio. Save these metrics beside the layer report and send them to W&B at every 10k gate. Use blank/nonblank and collapsed-sequence diagnostics to distinguish distribution calibration from peak-timing failure; normalized public English WER and Chinese CER remain the external promotion criteria.

- Stage210f factorized-CTC plan: preserve the BiRWKV/RWKV-7 encoder and frozen copied Nano five-layer decoder/head, but stop using a scalar nonblank frame multiplier as the main balancing mechanism. CTC KD studies identify sparse/arbitrary spike timing and blank dominance as separate failure modes; factorized progressive KD reports that blank/nonblank discrimination, balanced hidden transfer, and separately normalized posterior transfer outperform conventional CTC KD, while symmetric blank selection can support label-free KD. The project has already completed the representation stages and now observes substitution-dominated residuals plus insertion/deletion exchange. Add a backward-compatible online loss that (1) matches the binary teacher blank-versus-nonblank posterior on all valid frames, and (2) on teacher-top1 nonblank frames only, removes blank and ignored ids, renormalizes teacher and student over the valid nonblank vocabulary, and minimizes full-vocabulary conditional KL. Normalize the binary and token terms independently so `82%` blank frames cannot suppress token identity and the token term cannot change the student's blank logit. Retain a low uniform all-frame KL only as a distribution stabilizer, retain final-encoder/decoder-path hidden anchors, use zero weight decay, and keep all sequence/window/margin losses off in the first isolated admission. This follows the factorized/progressive result (`https://doi.org/10.1016/j.specom.2024.103071`) and the CTC spike-alignment diagnosis (`https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.08311`) without changing inference architecture. First run a short 4-GPU smoke and a fixed/public admission from the best Stage210e path checkpoint; promote to complete easy/medium/hard/long data only if direct Nano substitution falls without increasing insertion, blank MAE/nonblank ratio remain calibrated, and normalized AISHELL/LibriSpeech/Common Voice do not regress.
